% ----------------------
\subsection{Romans}
% ----------------------
	\label{ROMANS}
			
	% -----
	\subsubsection{Romans 1} % *ROMANS-1 
	% -----
		\label{ROMANS-1} 
		
		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 1:1} % *ROMANS-1-1 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-1-1}

			PAUL, a servant of Jesus Christ, called to be an apostle, separated unto the gospel of God,

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{Παῦλος δοῦλος Χριστοῦ Ἰησοῦ, κλητὸς ἀπόστολος, ἀφωρισμένος εἰς εὐαγγέλιον θεοῦ}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 1:2} % *ROMANS-1-2 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-1-2}

			(Which he had promised afore by his prophets in the holy scriptures,)

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ὃ προεπηγγείλατο διὰ τῶν προφητῶν αὐτοῦ ἐν γραφαῖς ἁγίαις}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 1:3} % *ROMANS-1-3 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-1-3}

			Concerning his Son Jesus Christ our Lord, which was made of the seed of David according to the flesh;

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{περὶ τοῦ υἱοῦ αὐτοῦ, τοῦ γενομένου ἐκ σπέρματος Δαυὶδ κατὰ σάρκα,}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 1:4} % *ROMANS-1-4 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-1-4}

			And declared%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					\gr{ὁρίζω}, so more like ``designated, appointed.''
					}
			to be the Son of God with power, according to the spirit of holiness, by the resurrection from the dead:

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{τοῦ ὁρισθέντος υἱοῦ θεοῦ ἐν δυνάμει κατὰ πνεῦμα ἁγιωσύνης ἐξ ἀναστάσεως νεκρῶν, Ἰησοῦ Χριστοῦ τοῦ κυρίου ἡμῶν,}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 1:5} % *ROMANS-1-5 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-1-5}

			By whom we have received grace and apostleship, for obedience to the faith among all nations, for his name:

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{δι᾽ οὗ ἐλάβομεν χάριν καὶ ἀποστολὴν εἰς ὑπακοὴν πίστεως ἐν πᾶσιν τοῖς ἔθνεσιν ὑπὲρ τοῦ ὀνόματος αὐτοῦ,}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 1:6} % *ROMANS-1-6 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-1-6}

			Among whom are ye also the called of Jesus Christ:

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἐν οἷς ἐστε καὶ ὑμεῖς κλητοὶ Ἰησοῦ Χριστοῦ,}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 1:7} % *ROMANS-1-7 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-1-7}

			To all that be in Rome, beloved of God, called to be saints: Grace to you and peace from God our Father, and the Lord Jesus Christ.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{πᾶσιν τοῖς οὖσιν ἐν Ῥώμῃ ἀγαπητοῖς θεοῦ, κλητοῖς ἁγίοις· χάρις ὑμῖν καὶ εἰρήνη ἀπὸ θεοῦ πατρὸς ἡμῶν καὶ κυρίου Ἰησοῦ Χριστοῦ.}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 1:8} % *ROMANS-1-8 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-1-8}

			First, I thank my God through Jesus Christ for you all, that your faith is spoken of throughout the whole world.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{Πρῶτον μὲν εὐχαριστῶ τῷ θεῷ μου διὰ Ἰησοῦ Χριστοῦ περὶ πάντων ὑμῶν, ὅτι ἡ πίστις ὑμῶν καταγγέλλεται ἐν ὅλῳ τῷ κόσμῳ.}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 1:9} % *ROMANS-1-9 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-1-9}

			For God is my witness, whom I serve with my spirit in the gospel of his Son, that without ceasing I make mention of you always in my prayers;

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{μάρτυς γάρ μού ἐστιν ὁ θεός, ᾧ λατρεύω ἐν τῷ πνεύματί μου ἐν τῷ εὐαγγελίῳ τοῦ υἱοῦ αὐτοῦ, ὡς ἀδιαλείπτως μνείαν ὑμῶν ποιοῦμαι}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 1:10} % *ROMANS-1-10 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-1-10}

			Making request, if by any means now at length I might have a prosperous journey by the will of God to come unto you.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{πάντοτε ἐπὶ τῶν προσευχῶν μου, δεόμενος εἴ πως ἤδη ποτὲ εὐοδωθήσομαι ἐν τῷ θελήματι τοῦ θεοῦ ἐλθεῖν πρὸς ὑμᾶς.}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 1:11} % *ROMANS-1-11 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-1-11}

			For I long to see you, that I may impart unto you some spiritual gift, to the end ye may be established;

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἐπιποθῶ γὰρ ἰδεῖν ὑμᾶς, ἵνα τι μεταδῶ χάρισμα ὑμῖν πνευματικὸν εἰς τὸ στηριχθῆναι ὑμᾶς,}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 1:12} % *ROMANS-1-12 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-1-12}

			That is, that I may be comforted together with you by the mutual faith both of you and me.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{τοῦτο δέ ἐστιν συμπαρακληθῆναι ἐν ὑμῖν διὰ τῆς ἐν ἀλλήλοις πίστεως ὑμῶν τε καὶ ἐμοῦ.}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 1:13} % *ROMANS-1-13 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-1-13}

			Now I would not have you ignorant, brethren, that oftentimes I purposed to come unto you, (but was let hitherto,) that I might have some fruit among you also, even as among other Gentiles.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{οὐ θέλω δὲ ὑμᾶς ἀγνοεῖν, ἀδελφοί, ὅτι πολλάκις προεθέμην ἐλθεῖν πρὸς ὑμᾶς, καὶ ἐκωλύθην ἄχρι τοῦ δεῦρο, ἵνα τινὰ καρπὸν σχῶ καὶ ἐν ὑμῖν καθὼς καὶ ἐν τοῖς λοιποῖς ἔθνεσιν.}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 1:14} % *ROMANS-1-14 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-1-14}

			I am debtor both to the Greeks, and to the Barbarians; both to the wise, and to the unwise.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{Ἕλλησίν τε καὶ βαρβάροις, σοφοῖς τε καὶ ἀνοήτοις ὀφειλέτης εἰμί·}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 1:15} % *ROMANS-1-15 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-1-15}

			So, as much as in me is, I am ready to preach the gospel to you that are at Rome also.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{οὕτως τὸ κατ᾽ ἐμὲ πρόθυμον καὶ ὑμῖν τοῖς ἐν Ῥώμῃ εὐαγγελίσασθαι.}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 1:16} % *ROMANS-1-16 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-1-16}

			For I am not ashamed of the gospel of Christ: for it is the power of God unto salvation to every one that believeth; to the Jew first, and also to the Greek.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{Οὐ γὰρ ἐπαισχύνομαι τὸ εὐαγγέλιον, δύναμις γὰρ θεοῦ ἐστιν εἰς σωτηρίαν παντὶ τῷ πιστεύοντι, Ἰουδαίῳ τε πρῶτον καὶ Ἕλληνι·}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 1:17} % *ROMANS-1-17 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-1-17}

			For therein%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					The opening part of this verse is a little confusing. It says, ``for the righteousness of God is revealed in the gospel on the basis of faith, for the purpose of faith.'' The beginning reminds me of \hyperref[DC84-20]{DC 84:20--22}, except there it's talking about the priesthood and ordinances. Righteousness is related to those things, though.
					}
			is the righteousness of God revealed from faith to faith: as it is written, The just shall live by faith.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{δικαιοσύνη γὰρ θεοῦ ἐν αὐτῷ ἀποκαλύπτεται ἐκ πίστεως εἰς πίστιν, καθὼς γέγραπται· Ὁ δὲ δίκαιος ἐκ πίστεως ζήσεται.}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 1:18} % *ROMANS-1-18 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-1-18}

			For the wrath of God is revealed from heaven against all ungodliness and unrighteousness of men, who hold%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					\gr{κατέχω}, so ``hold down, restrain, repress, stifle.'' These people repress the truth by means of their unrighteousness.
					}
			the truth in unrighteousness;

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{Ἀποκαλύπτεται γὰρ ὀργὴ θεοῦ ἀπ᾽ οὐρανοῦ ἐπὶ πᾶσαν ἀσέβειαν καὶ ἀδικίαν ἀνθρώπων τῶν τὴν ἀλήθειαν ἐν ἀδικίᾳ κατεχόντων,}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 1:19} % *ROMANS-1-19 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-1-19}

			Because that which may be known of God is manifest%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					Again, reminds me of \hyperref[DC84-20]{DC 84:20--22}, but note that we're not longer getting forms of \gr{ἀποκαλύπτω} here. Note that the next verse even uses \gr{θειότης}, which is a word that can definitely be taken as ``godliness.''
					}
			in them; for God hath shewed it unto them.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{διότι τὸ γνωστὸν τοῦ θεοῦ φανερόν ἐστιν ἐν αὐτοῖς, ὁ θεὸς γὰρ αὐτοῖς ἐφανέρωσεν.}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 1:20} % *ROMANS-1-20 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-1-20}

			For the invisible things of him from the creation of the world are clearly seen, being understood by the things that are made, even his eternal power and Godhead; so that they are without excuse:

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{τὰ γὰρ ἀόρατα αὐτοῦ ἀπὸ κτίσεως κόσμου τοῖς ποιήμασιν νοούμενα καθορᾶται, ἥ τε} \hyperref[AIDIOS]{\grl{ἀΐδιος}} \gr{αὐτοῦ δύναμις καὶ} \hyperref[THEIOTES]{\grl{θειότης}}\gr{, εἰς τὸ εἶναι αὐτοὺς ἀναπολογήτους,}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 1:21} % *ROMANS-1-21 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-1-21}

			Because that, when they knew God, they glorified him not as God, neither were thankful; but became vain in their imaginations, and their foolish heart was darkened.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{διότι γνόντες τὸν θεὸν οὐχ ὡς θεὸν ἐδόξασαν ἢ ηὐχαρίστησαν, ἀλλὰ ἐματαιώθησαν ἐν τοῖς διαλογισμοῖς αὐτῶν καὶ ἐσκοτίσθη ἡ ἀσύνετος αὐτῶν καρδία·}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 1:22} % *ROMANS-1-22 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-1-22}

			Professing themselves to be wise, they became fools,

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{φάσκοντες εἶναι σοφοὶ ἐμωράνθησαν,}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 1:23} % *ROMANS-1-23 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-1-23}

			And changed the glory of the uncorruptible God into an image made like to corruptible man, and to birds, and fourfooted beasts, and creeping things.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{καὶ ἤλλαξαν τὴν δόξαν τοῦ ἀφθάρτου θεοῦ ἐν ὁμοιώματι εἰκόνος φθαρτοῦ ἀνθρώπου καὶ πετεινῶν καὶ τετραπόδων καὶ ἑρπετῶν.}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 1:24} % *ROMANS-1-24 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-1-24}

			Wherefore God also gave them up to uncleanness through the lusts%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					Compare \hyperref[MATTHEW-5-28]{Matthew 5:28}.
					}
			of their own hearts, to dishonour their own bodies between themselves:

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}

			Who changed the truth of God into a lie, and worshipped and served the creature more than the Creator, who is blessed for ever. Amen.

			\begin{framed}

					\item[SBLG] \gr{οἵτινες μετήλλαξαν τὴν ἀλήθειαν τοῦ θεοῦ ἐν τῷ ψεύδει, καὶ ἐσεβάσθησαν καὶ ἐλάτρευσαν τῇ κτίσει παρὰ τὸν κτίσαντα, ὅς ἐστιν εὐλογητὸς εἰς τοὺς αἰῶνας· ἀμήν.}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 1:26} % *ROMANS-1-26 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-1-26}

				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					A few thoughts on these verses and their relation to gay people:
					\begin{compactitem}
						\item This verse and the next one make up one of the places in the NT where homosexuality has been thought to be condemned, at least traditionally. As of 10/22/23, I've only looked at two commentaries in detail, but both---the Romans NICNT volume by Moo, and the Romans NIGTC volume by Longenecker---say that Paul here is unequivocally condemning any kind of gay action or lifestyle. Longenecker engages a bit with exegetical attempts to soften the perceived condemnation, including a 1983 book by Scroggs called \textit{The New Testament and Homosexuality: Contextual Background for Contemporary Debate}, but rejects the main argument of these attempts, which is that Paul is only following his Hellenistic Jewish model of thinking about things and doesn't actually care that much in the end about anything gay. The attitude of both Moo and Longenecker is summed up by this remark by Moo, at NICNT 115 note 118: ``Scroggs's interpretation, and others like it, are vain attempts to avoid the obvious: Paul criticized homosexual activity as a particularly clear example of the extent to which people have fallen from a true knowledge of God.''
						\item On the other hand, in the twentieth century and now, given how contentious this issue is, it's impossible that any interpreter can go to the text without a preconceived opinion about what the right view is, and so every interpretation is just going to end up being a hodgepodge of motivated reasoning and confirmation bias.
						\item As usual, our responsibility is to examine the issue as broadly as possible, with all its connections and implications, and reach the view that best makes sense of everything and prioritizes the most fundamental values, inasmuch as this is possible.
						\item My own views on revelation suggest that, even if Paul is strongly condemning all things gay here---which, to be fair, it seems he is---there's no reason why this couldn't change. There hasn't been a revelation in the restoration about this, but there's no reason to think that there couldn't be.
					\end{compactitem}
					}%
			For this cause God gave them up unto vile affections: for even their women did change the natural use into that which is against nature:

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{Διὰ τοῦτο παρέδωκεν αὐτοὺς ὁ θεὸς εἰς πάθη ἀτιμίας· αἵ τε γὰρ θήλειαι αὐτῶν μετήλλαξαν τὴν φυσικὴν χρῆσιν εἰς τὴν παρὰ φύσιν,}
					% \item[AB ]

			For when the Gentiles, which have not the law, do by nature the things contained in the law, these, having not the law, are a law unto themselves:

			\begin{framed}

				\begin{compactdesc}

					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 2:15} % *ROMANS-2-15 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-2-15}

			Which shew the work of the law written in their hearts, their conscience also bearing witness, and their thoughts the mean while accusing or else excusing one another;)

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{οἵτινες ἐνδείκνυνται τὸ ἔργον τοῦ νόμου γραπτὸν ἐν ταῖς καρδίαις αὐτῶν, συμμαρτυρούσης αὐτῶν τῆς συνειδήσεως καὶ μεταξὺ ἀλλήλων τῶν λογισμῶν κατηγορούντων ἢ καὶ ἀπολογουμένων,}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					\item[ESV] They show that the work of the law is written on their hearts, while their conscience also bears witness, and their conflicting thoughts accuse or even excuse them
					\item[NASB] in that they show the work of the Law written in their hearts, their conscience bearing witness and their thoughts alternately accusing or else defending them,
					\item[NRSV] They show that what the law requires is written on their hearts, to which their own conscience also bears witness; and their conflicting thoughts will accuse or perhaps excuse them
					\item[NIRV] They show that what the law requires is written on their hearts. The way their minds judge them gives witness to that fact. Sometimes their thoughts find them guilty. At other times their thoughts find them not guilty.
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-2-16}

			In the day when God shall judge the secrets of men by Jesus Christ according to my gospel.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἐν ἡμέρᾳ ὅτε κρίνει ὁ θεὸς τὰ κρυπτὰ τῶν ἀνθρώπων κατὰ τὸ εὐαγγέλιόν μου διὰ Χριστοῦ Ἰησοῦ.}

		% ---

			Behold, thou art called a Jew, and restest in the law, and makest thy boast of God,

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{Εἰ δὲ σὺ Ἰουδαῖος ἐπονομάζῃ καὶ ἐπαναπαύῃ νόμῳ καὶ καυχᾶσαι ἐν θεῷ}

		% ---

			And knowest his will, and approvest the things that are more excellent, being instructed out of the law;

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{καὶ γινώσκεις τὸ θέλημα καὶ δοκιμάζεις τὰ διαφέροντα κατηχούμενος ἐκ τοῦ νόμου,}

		% ---

			And art confident that thou thyself art a guide of the blind, a light of them which are in darkness,

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{πέποιθάς τε σεαυτὸν ὁδηγὸν εἶναι τυφλῶν, φῶς τῶν ἐν σκότει,}

		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-2-20}

			An instructor of the foolish, a teacher of babes, which hast the form%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					Same word that you find at \hyperref[2TIMOTHY-3-5]{2 Timothy 3:5}.
					}
			of knowledge and of the truth in the law.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{παιδευτὴν ἀφρόνων, διδάσκαλον νηπίων, ἔχοντα τὴν μόρφωσιν τῆς γνώσεως καὶ τῆς ἀληθείας ἐν τῷ νόμῳ—}

		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-2-21}

			Thou therefore which teachest another, teachest thou not thyself? thou that preachest a man should not steal, dost thou steal?

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ὁ οὖν διδάσκων ἕτερον σεαυτὸν οὐ διδάσκεις; ὁ κηρύσσων μὴ κλέπτειν κλέπτεις;}

		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-2-22}

			Thou that sayest a man should not commit adultery, dost thou commit adultery? thou that abhorrest idols, dost thou commit sacrilege?

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ὁ λέγων μὴ μοιχεύειν μοιχεύεις; ὁ βδελυσσόμενος τὰ εἴδωλα ἱεροσυλεῖς;}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 2:23} % *ROMANS-2-23 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-2-23}

			Thou that makest thy boast of the law, through breaking the law dishonourest thou God?

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ὃς ἐν νόμῳ καυχᾶσαι, διὰ τῆς παραβάσεως τοῦ νόμου τὸν θεὸν ἀτιμάζεις;}

		\paragraph{Romans 2:24} % *ROMANS-2-24 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-2-24}

			For the name of God is blasphemed among the Gentiles through you, as it is written.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{τὸ γὰρ ὄνομα τοῦ θεοῦ δι᾽ ὑμᾶς βλασφημεῖται ἐν τοῖς ἔθνεσιν, καθὼς γέγραπται.}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 2:25} % *ROMANS-2-25 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-2-25}

			For circumcision verily profiteth, if thou keep the law: but if thou be a breaker of the law, thy circumcision is made uncircumcision.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{Περιτομὴ μὲν γὰρ ὠφελεῖ ἐὰν νόμον πράσσῃς· ἐὰν δὲ παραβάτης νόμου ᾖς, ἡ περιτομή σου ἀκροβυστία γέγονεν.}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 2:26} % *ROMANS-2-26 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-2-26}

			Therefore if the uncircumcision keep the righteousness of the law, shall not his uncircumcision be counted for circumcision?

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἐὰν οὖν ἡ ἀκροβυστία τὰ δικαιώματα τοῦ νόμου φυλάσσῃ, οὐχ ἡ ἀκροβυστία αὐτοῦ εἰς περιτομὴν λογισθήσεται;}

					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]

					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 2:27} % *ROMANS-2-27 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-2-27}

			And shall not uncircumcision which is by nature, if it fulfil the law, judge thee, who by the letter and circumcision dost transgress the law?

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{καὶ κρινεῖ ἡ ἐκ φύσεως ἀκροβυστία τὸν νόμον τελοῦσα σὲ τὸν διὰ γράμματος καὶ περιτομῆς παραβάτην νόμου.}

					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]

					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 2:28} % *ROMANS-2-28 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-2-28}

			For he is not a Jew, which is one outwardly; neither is that circumcision, which is outward in the flesh:

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{οὐ γὰρ ὁ ἐν τῷ φανερῷ Ἰουδαῖός ἐστιν, οὐδὲ ἡ ἐν τῷ φανερῷ ἐν σαρκὶ περιτομή·}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]

					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 2:29} % *ROMANS-2-29 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-2-29}

			But he is a Jew, which is one inwardly; and circumcision is that of the heart, in the spirit, and not in the letter; whose praise is not of men, but of God.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἀλλ᾽ ὁ ἐν τῷ κρυπτῷ Ἰουδαῖος, καὶ περιτομὴ καρδίας ἐν πνεύματι οὐ γράμματι, οὗ ὁ ἔπαινος οὐκ ἐξ ἀνθρώπων ἀλλ᾽ ἐκ τοῦ θεοῦ.}

					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]

				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

	% -----
	\subsubsection{Romans 3} % *ROMANS-3 
	% -----
		\label{ROMANS-3}
		
		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 3:1} % *ROMANS-3-1 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-3-1}

			WHAT advantage then hath the Jew? or what profit is there of circumcision?

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{Τί οὖν τὸ περισσὸν τοῦ Ἰουδαίου, ἢ τίς ἡ ὠφέλεια τῆς περιτομῆς;}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 3:2} % *ROMANS-3-2 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-3-2}

			Much every way: chiefly, because that unto them were committed the oracles of God.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{πολὺ κατὰ πάντα τρόπον. πρῶτον μὲν γὰρ ὅτι ἐπιστεύθησαν τὰ λόγια τοῦ θεοῦ.}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 3:3} % *ROMANS-3-3 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-3-3}

			For what if some did not believe? shall their unbelief make the faith of God without effect?

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{τί γάρ; εἰ ἠπίστησάν τινες, μὴ ἡ ἀπιστία αὐτῶν τὴν πίστιν τοῦ θεοῦ καταργήσει;}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 3:4} % *ROMANS-3-4 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-3-4}

			God forbid: yea, let God be true, but every man a liar; as it is written, That thou mightest be justified in thy sayings, and mightest overcome when thou art judged.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{μὴ γένοιτο· γινέσθω δὲ ὁ θεὸς ἀληθής, πᾶς δὲ ἄνθρωπος ψεύστης, καθὼς γέγραπται· Ὅπως ἂν δικαιωθῇς ἐν τοῖς λόγοις σου καὶ νικήσεις ἐν τῷ κρίνεσθαί σε.}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 3:5} % *ROMANS-3-5 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-3-5}

			But if our unrighteousness commend the righteousness of God, what shall we say? Is God unrighteous who taketh vengeance? (I speak as a man)

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{εἰ δὲ ἡ ἀδικία ἡμῶν θεοῦ δικαιοσύνην συνίστησιν, τί ἐροῦμεν; μὴ ἄδικος ὁ θεὸς ὁ ἐπιφέρων τὴν ὀργήν; κατὰ ἄνθρωπον λέγω.}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 3:6} % *ROMANS-3-6 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-3-6}

			God forbid: for then how shall God judge the world?

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{μὴ γένοιτο· ἐπεὶ πῶς κρινεῖ ὁ θεὸς τὸν κόσμον;}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 3:7} % *ROMANS-3-7 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-3-7}

			For if the truth of God hath more abounded through my lie unto his glory; why yet am I also judged as a sinner?

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{εἰ δὲ ἡ ἀλήθεια τοῦ θεοῦ ἐν τῷ ἐμῷ ψεύσματι ἐπερίσσευσεν εἰς τὴν δόξαν αὐτοῦ, τί ἔτι κἀγὼ ὡς ἁμαρτωλὸς κρίνομαι,}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 3:8} % *ROMANS-3-8 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-3-8}

			And not rather, (as we be slanderously reported, and as some affirm that we say,) Let us do evil, that good may come? whose damnation is just.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{καὶ μὴ καθὼς βλασφημούμεθα καὶ καθώς φασίν τινες ἡμᾶς λέγειν ὅτι Ποιήσωμεν τὰ κακὰ ἵνα ἔλθῃ τὰ ἀγαθά; ὧν τὸ κρίμα ἔνδικόν ἐστιν.}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 3:9} % *ROMANS-3-9 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-3-9}

			What then? are we better than they? No, in no wise: for we have before proved both Jews and Gentiles, that they are all under sin;%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					For the phrase ``under sin,'' see \hyperref[SIN-PHRASES]{here}.
					}

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{Τί οὖν; προεχόμεθα; οὐ πάντως, προῃτιασάμεθα γὰρ Ἰουδαίους τε καὶ Ἕλληνας πάντας ὑφ᾽ ἁμαρτίαν εἶναι,}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]

		% ---

			Now we know that what things soever the law saith, it saith to them who are under the law: that every mouth may be stopped, and all the world may become guilty before God.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{Οἴδαμεν δὲ ὅτι ὅσα ὁ νόμος λέγει τοῖς ἐν τῷ νόμῳ λαλεῖ, ἵνα πᾶν στόμα φραγῇ καὶ ὑπόδικος γένηται πᾶς ὁ κόσμος τῷ θεῷ·}

		% ---

			Therefore by the deeds of the law there shall no flesh be justified in his sight: for by the law is the knowledge of sin.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{διότι ἐξ ἔργων νόμου οὐ δικαιωθήσεται πᾶσα σὰρξ ἐνώπιον αὐτοῦ, διὰ γὰρ νόμου ἐπίγνωσις ἁμαρτίας.}

		% ---

			But now the righteousness of God without the law is manifested, being witnessed by the law and the prophets;

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{Νυνὶ δὲ χωρὶς νόμου δικαιοσύνη θεοῦ πεφανέρωται, μαρτυρουμένη ὑπὸ τοῦ νόμου καὶ τῶν προφητῶν,}

		% ---

			Even the righteousness of God which is by faith of Jesus Christ unto all and upon all them that believe: for there is no difference:

			\begin{framed}

					\item[SBLG] \gr{δικαιοσύνη δὲ θεοῦ διὰ πίστεως Ἰησοῦ Χριστοῦ, εἰς πάντας τοὺς πιστεύοντας, οὐ γάρ ἐστιν διαστολή.}

					For similar ideas, see \hyperref[GALATIANS-3-22]{Galatians 3:22},
					}%
			For all have sinned, and come short of the glory of God;

		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-3-24}

			Being justified freely by his grace through the redemption that is in Christ Jesus:

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{δικαιούμενοι δωρεὰν τῇ αὐτοῦ χάριτι διὰ τῆς ἀπολυτρώσεως τῆς ἐν Χριστῷ Ἰησοῦ·}

					% \item[NTR1]

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 3:25} % *ROMANS-3-25 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-3-25}

			Whom God hath set forth to be a \hyperref[PROPITIATION]{propitiation}%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					The English word ``propitiation'' appears three times in the KJV: here, \hyperref[1JOHN-2-2]{1 John 2:2}, and \hyperref[1JOHN-4-10]{1 John 4:10}. Here the word is \gr{ἱλαστήριον} (BDAG 420a); in the 1 John verses it's \gr{ἱλασμός} (BDAG 420a).
					}
			through faith in his blood, to declare his righteousness for the remission of sins that are past, through the forbearance of God;

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ὃν προέθετο ὁ θεὸς ἱλαστήριον διὰ πίστεως ἐν τῷ αὐτοῦ αἵματι εἰς ἔνδειξιν τῆς δικαιοσύνης αὐτοῦ διὰ τὴν πάρεσιν τῶν προγεγονότων ἁμαρτημάτων}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 3:26} % *ROMANS-3-26 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-3-26}

			To declare, I say, at this time his righteousness: that he might be just, and the justifier of him which believeth in Jesus.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἐν τῇ ἀνοχῇ τοῦ θεοῦ, πρὸς τὴν ἔνδειξιν τῆς δικαιοσύνης αὐτοῦ ἐν τῷ νῦν καιρῷ, εἰς τὸ εἶναι αὐτὸν δίκαιον καὶ δικαιοῦντα τὸν ἐκ πίστεως Ἰησοῦ.}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 3:27} % *ROMANS-3-27 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-3-27}

			Where is boasting then? It is excluded. By what law? of works? Nay: but by the law of faith.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{Ποῦ οὖν ἡ καύχησις; ἐξεκλείσθη. διὰ ποίου νόμου; τῶν ἔργων; οὐχί, ἀλλὰ διὰ νόμου πίστεως.}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 3:28} % *ROMANS-3-28 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-3-28}

			Therefore we conclude that a man is justified by faith without the deeds of the law.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{λογιζόμεθα γὰρ δικαιοῦσθαι πίστει ἄνθρωπον χωρὶς ἔργων νόμου.}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 3:29} % *ROMANS-3-29 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-3-29}

			Is he the God of the Jews only? is he not also of the Gentiles? Yes, of the Gentiles also:

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἢ Ἰουδαίων ὁ θεὸς μόνον; οὐχὶ καὶ ἐθνῶν; ναὶ καὶ ἐθνῶν,}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 3:30} % *ROMANS-3-30 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-3-30}

			Seeing it is one God, which shall justify the circumcision by faith, and uncircumcision through faith.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{εἴπερ εἷς ὁ θεός, ὃς δικαιώσει περιτομὴν ἐκ πίστεως καὶ ἀκροβυστίαν διὰ τῆς πίστεως.}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 3:31} % *ROMANS-3-31 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-3-31}

			Do we then make void the law through faith? God forbid: yea, we establish%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					\gr{ἱστάνω}; BDAG 426b, this use listed under meaning \#A.4, ``\textbf{to validate something that is in force or in practice, \textit{reinforce validity of, uphold, maintain, validate}}.'' It also lists \hyperref[MARK-7-9]{Mark 7:9} here, and mentions \gr{καταργέω}, the word at the beginning of the verse, which looks like it's supposed to be the opposite idea.
					}
			the law.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{νόμον οὖν καταργοῦμεν διὰ τῆς πίστεως; μὴ γένοιτο, ἀλλὰ νόμον ἱστάνομεν.}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

	% -----
	\subsubsection{Romans 4} % *ROMANS-4 
	% -----
		\label{ROMANS-4}
		
		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 4:1} % *ROMANS-4-1 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-4-1}

			WHAT shall we say then that Abraham our father, as pertaining to the flesh, hath found?

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{Τί οὖν ἐροῦμεν εὑρηκέναι Ἀβραὰμ τὸν προπάτορα ἡμῶν κατὰ σάρκα;}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 4:2} % *ROMANS-4-2 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-4-2}

			For if Abraham were justified by works, he hath whereof to glory; but not before God.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{εἰ γὰρ Ἀβραὰμ ἐξ ἔργων ἐδικαιώθη, ἔχει καύχημα· ἀλλ᾽ οὐ πρὸς θεόν,}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 4:3} % *ROMANS-4-3 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-4-3}

			For what saith the scripture?%
				\footnote{%
					\label{ROMANS-4-3-scripture}%
					``The scripture'' here refers to the OT (AB-1993 373). Paul is referring to \hyperref[GENESIS-15-6]{Genesis 15:6} here; he's going to quote from the LXX and the Greek text there matches the Greek text here almost exactly, with the main difference being that the LXX uses \gr{Αβραμ} instead of \gr{Ἀβραὰμ} as we see here. See also \hyperref[GALATIANS-3-6]{Galatians 3:6}, where he makes the same quotation.
					}
			Abraham believed God, and it was counted%
				\footnote{%
					\label{ROMANS-4-3-counted}%
					The ``counted unto'' construction occurs in the BOM, in \hyperref[MORONI-7-7]{Moroni 7:7--9}. Here it's various forms of \gr{λογίζομαι}; see the definitions of that word \hyperref[LOGIZOMAI]{here}. Note that the KJV also uses a translate of ``impute'' for this word, and that the word occurs many times in this chapter, not just here. The next occurrence, for example, is in the very next verse.
					}
			unto him for righteousness.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{τί γὰρ ἡ γραφὴ λέγει; Ἐπίστευσεν δὲ Ἀβραὰμ τῷ θεῷ καὶ} \hyperref[LOGIZOMAI]{\grl{ἐλογίσθη}} \gr{αὐτῷ εἰς δικαιοσύνην.}
					\item[AB-1993 369] For what does Scripture say? \textit{Abraham put his faith in God, and it was credited to him as uprightness}.
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 4:17} % *ROMANS-4-17 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-4-17}

			(As it is written, I have made thee a father of many nations,) before him whom he believed, even God, who quickeneth the dead,
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					See NICNT Romans 281--282 for this unusual bit about ``calling'': ``In the OT, the verb `call' refers to God's creative work (cf. \hyperref[ISAIAH-41-4]{Isa. 41:4}; \hyperref[ISAIAH-48-13]{48:13}), and later Jewish authors perpetuate this usage, sometimes adding the idea that this creative `calling' involves a bringing into being things that were not. It may be, then, this tradition of a \textit{creatio ex nihilo} to which Paul alludes, with the purpose of reminding his readers of God's creative power generally. Moreover, several texts apply this language to spiritual conversion, and sometimes, as here, with reference to God's giving life to the dead. These parallels make it possible that Paul's description of God as `the one who calls into being things that are not' is a reminder that God `justifies the ungodly' (v. 5): he creates out of the nothingness of people's empty, sinful lives a new, vibrant, spiritual life.''
					}%
			and calleth those things which be not as though they were.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{καθὼς γέγραπται ὅτι Πατέρα πολλῶν ἐθνῶν τέθεικά σε), κατέναντι οὗ ἐπίστευσεν θεοῦ τοῦ ζῳοποιοῦντος τοὺς νεκροὺς καὶ καλοῦντος τὰ μὴ ὄντα ὡς ὄντα·}

	\subsubsection{Romans 5} % *ROMANS-5 
	% -----
		\label{ROMANS-5}
		
		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 5:1} % *ROMANS-5-1 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-5-1}

			THEREFORE being justified by faith, we have peace with God through our Lord Jesus Christ:

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}

		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-5-2}

			By whom also we have access by faith into this grace wherein we stand, and rejoice in hope of the glory of God.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{δι᾽ οὗ καὶ τὴν προσαγωγὴν ἐσχήκαμεν τῇ πίστει εἰς τὴν χάριν ταύτην ἐν ᾗ ἑστήκαμεν, καὶ καυχώμεθα ἐπ᾽ ἐλπίδι τῆς δόξης τοῦ θεοῦ·}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 5:3} % *ROMANS-5-3 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-5-3}

			And not only so, but we glory in tribulations also: knowing that tribulation worketh
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					\gr{ὑπομονή}, so ``endurance, perseverance.'' Related to \hyperref[HUPOMENO]{\grl{ὑπομένω}}.
					}%
			patience;

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{οὐ μόνον δέ, ἀλλὰ καὶ καυχώμεθα ἐν ταῖς θλίψεσιν, εἰδότες ὅτι ἡ θλῖψις ὑπομονὴν κατεργάζεται,}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]

					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 5:4} % *ROMANS-5-4 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-5-4}

			And patience, experience;%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					The word here is \gr{δοκιμή}. BDAG 226b meaning \#1 is ``\textbf{a testing process, \textit{test, ordeal}},'' but this verse is listed under the second and final meaning, ``\textbf{the experience of going through a test with special reference to the result, \textit{standing a test, character}}.'' About this verse BDAG adds: ``(a pregnant constr.: \gr{ὑπομονή} as a process of enduring something amounts to a test that promotes and validates the character of the one undergoing it. This success in turn promotes \gr{ἐλπίς}.'' LSJ doesn't add much more---meaning \#1 is ``\textit{proof, test},'' and \#2 is ``\textit{tried} or \textit{approved character},'' and it gives to NT verses.
					}
			and experience, hope:

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἡ δὲ ὑπομονὴ δοκιμήν, ἡ δὲ δοκιμὴ ἐλπίδα.}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]

			\end{framed}

		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-5-5}

			And hope maketh not ashamed;%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					\gr{καταισχύνω}---BDAG 458a glosses this part as ``hope does not disappoint.'' 
					}
			because the love of God is shed abroad in our hearts by the Holy Ghost which is given unto us.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἡ δὲ ἐλπὶς οὐ καταισχύνει· ὅτι ἡ ἀγάπη τοῦ θεοῦ} \hyperref[EKCHEO]{\grl{ἐκκέχυται}} \gr{ἐν ταῖς καρδίαις ἡμῶν διὰ πνεύματος ἁγίου τοῦ δοθέντος ἡμῖν.}

			For scarcely for a righteous man will one die: yet peradventure for a good man some would even dare to die.

					\item[SBLG] \gr{μόλις γὰρ ὑπὲρ δικαίου τις ἀποθανεῖται· ὑπὲρ γὰρ τοῦ ἀγαθοῦ τάχα τις καὶ τολμᾷ ἀποθανεῖν·}

					\item[SBLG] \gr{συνίστησιν δὲ τὴν ἑαυτοῦ ἀγάπην εἰς ἡμᾶς ὁ θεὸς ὅτι ἔτι ἁμαρτωλῶν ὄντων ἡμῶν Χριστὸς ὑπὲρ ἡμῶν ἀπέθανεν.}

		% ---

			Much more then, being now justified by his blood, we shall be saved from wrath through him.

					\item[SBLG] \gr{πολλῷ οὖν μᾶλλον δικαιωθέντες νῦν ἐν τῷ αἵματι αὐτοῦ σωθησόμεθα δι᾽ αὐτοῦ ἀπὸ τῆς ὀργῆς.}

		% ---

			For if, when we were enemies, we were reconciled to God by the death of his Son, much more, being reconciled, we shall be saved by his life.

					\item[SBLG] \gr{εἰ γὰρ ἐχθροὶ ὄντες κατηλλάγημεν τῷ θεῷ διὰ τοῦ θανάτου τοῦ υἱοῦ αὐτοῦ, πολλῷ μᾶλλον καταλλαγέντες σωθησόμεθα ἐν τῇ ζωῇ αὐτοῦ·}

		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-5-11}

			And not only so, but we also joy in God through our Lord Jesus Christ, by whom we have now received the atonement.

				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{οὐ μόνον δέ, ἀλλὰ καὶ καυχώμενοι ἐν τῷ θεῷ διὰ τοῦ κυρίου ἡμῶν Ἰησοῦ Χριστοῦ, δι᾽ οὗ νῦν τὴν} \hyperref[KATALLAGE]{\grl{καταλλαγὴν}} \gr{ἐλάβομεν.}

		\paragraph{Romans 5:12} % *ROMANS-5-12 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-5-12}

			Wherefore, as by one man sin entered into the world, and death by sin; and so death passed upon all men, for that all have sinned:

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{Διὰ τοῦτο ὥσπερ δι᾽ ἑνὸς ἀνθρώπου ἡ ἁμαρτία εἰς τὸν κόσμον εἰσῆλθεν καὶ διὰ τῆς ἁμαρτίας ὁ θάνατος, καὶ οὕτως εἰς πάντας ἀνθρώπους ὁ θάνατος διῆλθεν ἐφ᾽ ᾧ πάντες ἥμαρτον—}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]

			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 5:13} % *ROMANS-5-13
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-5-13}
			
			(For until the law sin was in the world: but sin is not imputed%
				\footnote{%
					\label{ROMANS-5-13-imputed}%
					Note that this use of ``imputed'' is \textit{not} \gr{λογίζομαι}, as it sometimes is in \hyperref[ROMANS-4]{Romans 4}. There are no variant readings that have \gr{λογίζομαι}. For this verb here, \gr{ἐλλογέω}, AB-1993 417 says, ``Paul enunciates a general principle that agrees with 4:15 and 3:20 and presupposes the Jewish conception of heavenly books in which human deeds were recorded (see the NOTE on 4:3). He uses again the bookkeeping vb. \textit{ellogeitai}, `charge to someone's account'...Sin neither in its grossness nor in its quantity was booked against individuals. There could be no transgression where there was no law.''
					}
			when there is no law.
			
			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἄχρι γὰρ νόμου ἁμαρτία ἦν ἐν κόσμῳ, ἁμαρτία δὲ οὐκ ἐλλογεῖται μὴ ὄντος νόμου,}
					\item[AB-1993 ] up to the time of the law sin was in the world, even though sin is not accounted when there is no law;
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}
		
		% --
		\paragraph{Romans 5:14}
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-5-14}
		
			Nevertheless death reigned from Adam to Moses, even over them that had not sinned after the similitude of Adam's transgression, who is the figure of him that was to come.
			
			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἀλλὰ ἐβασίλευσεν ὁ θάνατος ἀπὸ Ἀδὰμ μέχρι Μωϋσέως καὶ ἐπὶ τοὺς μὴ ἁμαρτήσαντας ἐπὶ τῷ ὁμοιώματι τῆς παραβάσεως Ἀδάμ, ὅς ἐστιν τύπος τοῦ μέλλοντος.}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 5:15} % *ROMANS-5-15 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-5-15}

			But not as the offence, so also is the free gift. For if through the offence of one many be dead, much more the grace of God, and the gift by grace, which is by one man, Jesus Christ, hath abounded unto many.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{Ἀλλ᾽ οὐχ ὡς τὸ παράπτωμα, οὕτως καὶ τὸ χάρισμα· εἰ γὰρ τῷ τοῦ ἑνὸς παραπτώματι οἱ πολλοὶ ἀπέθανον, πολλῷ μᾶλλον ἡ χάρις τοῦ θεοῦ καὶ ἡ δωρεὰ ἐν χάριτι τῇ τοῦ ἑνὸς ἀνθρώπου Ἰησοῦ Χριστοῦ εἰς τοὺς πολλοὺς ἐπερίσσευσεν.}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 5:16} % *ROMANS-5-16 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-5-16}

			And not as it was by one that sinned, so is the gift: for the judgment was by one to condemnation, but the free gift is of many offences unto \hyperref[JUSTIFICATION]{justification}.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{καὶ οὐχ ὡς δι᾽ ἑνὸς ἁμαρτήσαντος τὸ δώρημα· τὸ μὲν γὰρ κρίμα ἐξ ἑνὸς εἰς κατάκριμα, τὸ δὲ χάρισμα ἐκ πολλῶν παραπτωμάτων εἰς δικαίωμα.}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 5:17} % *ROMANS-5-17 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-5-17}

			For if by one man's offence death reigned by one; much more they which receive abundance of grace and of the gift of righteousness shall reign in life by one, Jesus Christ.)

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{εἰ γὰρ τῷ τοῦ ἑνὸς παραπτώματι ὁ θάνατος ἐβασίλευσεν διὰ τοῦ ἑνός, πολλῷ μᾶλλον οἱ τὴν περισσείαν τῆς χάριτος καὶ τῆς δωρεᾶς τῆς δικαιοσύνης λαμβάνοντες ἐν ζωῇ βασιλεύσουσιν διὰ τοῦ ἑνὸς Ἰησοῦ Χριστοῦ.}

					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]

					% \item[NTXXXXX*]

		% ---
		\paragraph{Romans 5:18} % *ROMANS-5-18 
		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-5-18}

			Therefore as by the offence of one judgment came upon all men to condemnation; even so by the righteousness of one the free gift came upon all men unto \hyperref[JUSTIFICATION]{justification} of life.

			\begin{framed}
				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{Ἄρα οὖν ὡς δι᾽ ἑνὸς παραπτώματος εἰς πάντας ἀνθρώπους εἰς κατάκριμα, οὕτως καὶ δι᾽ ἑνὸς δικαιώματος εἰς πάντας ἀνθρώπους εἰς δικαίωσιν ζωῆς·}
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-5-19}

			For as by one man's disobedience many were made sinners, so by the obedience of one shall many be made righteous.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ὥσπερ γὰρ διὰ τῆς παρακοῆς τοῦ ἑνὸς ἀνθρώπου ἁμαρτωλοὶ κατεστάθησαν οἱ πολλοί, οὕτως καὶ διὰ τῆς ὑπακοῆς τοῦ ἑνὸς δίκαιοι κατασταθήσονται οἱ πολλοί.}

					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]

					% \item[NTXXXXX*]

		% ---

			Moreover the law entered, that the offence might abound. But where sin abounded, grace did much more abound:

				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{νόμος δὲ παρεισῆλθεν ἵνα πλεονάσῃ τὸ παράπτωμα· οὗ δὲ ἐπλεόνασεν ἡ ἁμαρτία, ὑπερεπερίσσευσεν ἡ χάρις,}

		% ---
			\label{ROMANS-5-21}

			That as sin hath reigned unto death, even so might grace reign through righteousness unto eternal life by Jesus Christ our Lord.

			\begin{framed}
